Yeah… I have. I used to be pretty good about making time for things, like hiking on weekends, yoga classes, messing around in my garden. I even used to take my camera out for bird photos—there’s this little spot where I’d always try to catch cardinals, though honestly my pictures were never as good as I thought they were.

But lately I just… don’t go. I cancel plans or tell myself I’ll do it next weekend, and then the camera just sits in the closet. Even the garden, which used to make me feel better, feels like another chore. It’s not that I hate those things now, it’s more like none of it feels worth the effort.

I cancel plans or tell myself I’ll do it next weekend, and then the camera just sits in the closet. Even the garden, which used to make me feel better, feels like another chore. It’s not that I hate those things now, it’s more like none of it feels worth the effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Sometimes… yeah. I’ll start thinking about stupid things too, like whether I paid the electric bill or if the dishwasher is making that weird noise again, and then it circles right back to… that thought. It keeps coming back enough that it scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you made a plan to kill yourself?\n\nPatient: No… no, I haven’t made a plan. I’m… not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if it’s “trouble sleeping” in the way you mean.

Even folding laundry feels like this huge project.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days I wake up and there’s just this heavy, flat feeling in my chest, like before anything has even happened. I’ll go sit at the kitchen table with my coffee and end up just staring out at the yard, and it’s like… I can’t picture things getting better. Like there isn’t some good version of the future waiting, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic. The yard’s actually a mess right now too—I keep meaning to do something with the flower beds, my neighbor has these perfect hydrangeas and mine are just… sad. But yeah, underneath all that, I’ve been feeling really down.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I used to be pretty good about making time for things, like hiking on weekends, yoga classes, messing around in my garden. I even used to take my camera out for bird photos—there’s this little spot where I’d always try to catch cardinals, though honestly my pictures were never as good as I thought they were.\n\nBut lately I just… don’t go. I cancel plans or tell myself I’ll do it next weekend, and then the camera just sits in the closet. Even the garden, which used to make me feel better, feels like another chore. It’s not that I hate those things now, it’s more like none of it feels worth the effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Sometimes… yeah. Not like I’ve sat down and planned anything, I haven’t. But late at night is when it gets really bad—my husband will be asleep, and I’ll just be lying there thinking maybe he’d be better off without me.\n\nAnd then I feel awful for even thinking that, because he’s a good person and he’d probably be horrified if he knew. I’ll start thinking about stupid things too, like whether I paid the electric bill or if the dishwasher is making that weird noise again, and then it circles right back to… that thought. It keeps coming back enough that it scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you made a plan to kill yourself?\n\nPatient: No… no, I haven’t made a plan. It’s more that late at night, when everything’s quiet, I catch myself thinking my husband might be better off without me. And then I’ll get stuck on it, even if I try to think about something else—like whether I left dishes in the sink or if I forgot to buy coffee—and somehow it comes back to that thought.\n\nBut I haven’t planned anything. It just scares me that it keeps showing up.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time in the past when you went through a similar period of feeling this depressed?\n\nPatient: I think so… I mean, not exactly the same, but I’ve had stretches where it felt like this. Most days now I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling in my chest, like before I even get out of bed I already know the day is going to be gray.\n\nSometimes I’ll just sit at the kitchen table and stare out at the yard—there’s this one patch where the grass never really comes in right, no matter what I do, which is ridiculous because I’ve tried seed and those little sprinkler timers—and I’ll catch myself thinking there’s no good version of the future coming. Like I can’t picture things turning around.\n\nSo… yes, I guess I have felt this kind of low before, but this current stretch feels pretty hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Before this current stretch, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I know I’ve had low times before, but whether it was definitely two full weeks… I don’t know, I don’t keep track very well.\n\nI can remember thinking, “I’ve been dragging for a while,” but then I get mixed up because work would get busy, or I’d be focused on some report deadline, or my mom would call about something with her house, and I’d just sort of push through. So… maybe? But I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that you snore loudly or stop breathing in your sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I’m not really sure. My husband hasn’t said anything like that to me, I don’t think. I guess I wouldn’t know unless he told me, right?\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever a time when you felt depressed or lost interest in things nearly every day for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I remember times where I’d lose interest in things and feel really low, but pinning it down to “nearly every day” and “two weeks” is hard for me.\n\nI wasn’t writing it on a calendar or anything. And sometimes I’d still go to work and answer emails and do the grocery list, so it’s like… does that count? I remember one winter I barely wanted to do anything after work, but then again it was cold and dark and we were dealing with that weird issue with the furnace—sorry, that’s probably not what you’re asking.\n\nSo… maybe, but I can’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if it’s “trouble sleeping” in the way you mean.\n\nSome nights I’m up later than I planned, but then that can happen because I get caught up scrolling or I remember some random thing I forgot to do—like last night I started thinking about whether I’d paid the water bill, and then somehow I was looking at kitchen organizers online. So I don’t know if that counts as not sleeping or just me being bad at going to bed.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed my sleep feels kind of off, but I haven’t really tracked it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about everyday things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it excessive. I mean, I worry about regular things—work stuff, bills, whether I forgot to reply to someone. I’ll go sit at the kitchen table with my coffee and end up just staring out at the yard, and it’s like… I can’t picture things getting better. Like there isn’t some good version of the future waiting, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic. The yard’s actually a mess right now too—I keep meaning to do something with the flower beds, my neighbor has these perfect hydrangeas and mine are just… sad. But yeah, underneath all that, I’ve been feeling really down.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I used to be pretty good about making time for things, like hiking on weekends, yoga classes, messing around in my garden. I even used to take my camera out for bird photos—there’s this little spot where I’d always try to catch cardinals, though honestly my pictures were never as good as I thought they were.\n\nBut lately I just… don’t go. I cancel plans or tell myself I’ll do it next weekend, and then the camera just sits in the closet. Even the garden, which used to make me feel better, feels like another chore. It’s not that I hate those things now, it’s more like none of it feels worth the effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Sometimes… yeah. Not like I’ve sat down and planned anything, I haven’t. But late at night is when it gets really bad—my husband will be asleep, and I’ll just be lying there thinking maybe he’d be better off without me.\n\nAnd then I feel awful for even thinking that, because he’s a good person and he’d probably be horrified if he knew. I’ll start thinking about stupid things too, like whether I paid the electric bill or if the dishwasher is making that weird noise again, and then it circles right back to… that thought. It keeps coming back enough that it scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you made a plan to kill yourself?\n\nPatient: No… no, I haven’t made a plan. It’s more that late at night, when everything’s quiet, I catch myself thinking my husband might be better off without me. And then I’ll get stuck on it, even if I try to think about something else—like whether I left dishes in the sink or if I forgot to buy coffee—and somehow it comes back to that thought.\n\nBut I haven’t planned anything. It just scares me that it keeps showing up.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time in the past when you went through a similar period of feeling this depressed?\n\nPatient: I think so… I mean, not exactly the same, but I’ve had stretches where it felt like this. Most days now I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling in my chest, like before I even get out of bed I already know the day is going to be gray.\n\nSometimes I’ll just sit at the kitchen table and stare out at the yard—there’s this one patch where the grass never really comes in right, no matter what I do, which is ridiculous because I’ve tried seed and those little sprinkler timers—and I’ll catch myself thinking there’s no good version of the future coming. Like I can’t picture things turning around.\n\nSo… yes, I guess I have felt this kind of low before, but this current stretch feels pretty hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Before this current stretch, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I know I’ve had low times before, but whether it was definitely two full weeks… I don’t know, I don’t keep track very well.\n\nI can remember thinking, “I’ve been dragging for a while,” but then I get mixed up because work would get busy, or I’d be focused on some report deadline, or my mom would call about something with her house, and I’d just sort of push through. So… maybe? But I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that you snore loudly or stop breathing in your sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I’m not really sure. My husband hasn’t said anything like that to me, I don’t think. I mean, sometimes he’ll joke that I breathe “dramatically” when I’m annoyed, but that’s during the day, not sleeping.\n\nI haven’t woken up to him shaking me or saying I stopped breathing or anything. I guess I wouldn’t know unless he told me, right?\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever a time when you felt depressed or lost interest in things nearly every day for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I remember times where I’d lose interest in things and feel really low, but pinning it down to “nearly every day” and “two weeks” is hard for me.\n\nI wasn’t writing it on a calendar or anything. And sometimes I’d still go to work and answer emails and do the grocery list, so it’s like… does that count? I remember one winter I barely wanted to do anything after work, but then again it was cold and dark and we were dealing with that weird issue with the furnace—sorry, that’s probably not what you’re asking.\n\nSo… maybe, but I can’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if it’s “trouble sleeping” in the way you mean.\n\nSome nights I’m up later than I planned, but then that can happen because I get caught up scrolling or I remember some random thing I forgot to do—like last night I started thinking about whether I’d paid the water bill, and then somehow I was looking at kitchen organizers online. So I don’t know if that counts as not sleeping or just me being bad at going to bed.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed my sleep feels kind of off, but I haven’t really tracked it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about everyday things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it excessive. I mean, I worry about regular things—work stuff, bills, whether I forgot to reply to someone. But I feel like everyone does that, right?\n\nSometimes I’ll get stuck on something small, like whether I said the wrong thing in an email, and then I’m thinking about it while I’m unloading the dishwasher, and then I notice the mugs are all in the wrong cabinet because my husband puts them wherever—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nI guess I’m not sure where the line is between normal worrying and “excessive.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually cold when other people seem comfortable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean, I’m cold sometimes, but I’m usually the person who brings a sweater everywhere anyway. Like restaurants are always freezing to me, especially those ones with the vents right over the booth—there’s this place near my office where I swear they keep it like a meat locker.\n\nBut compared to other people… I haven’t really noticed anything unusual. My husband runs warm, so he’s not a great comparison. So I’d say no, not really, or at least not that I’ve paid attention to.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up in the morning, do you usually feel rested?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Even if I’ve been in bed a full night, I wake up feeling like I didn’t get restored, if that makes sense. Like my body is just… heavy. Sandbags is what it feels like sometimes.\n\nAnd then I’m trying to get through work, staring at spreadsheets or survey results, and it’s not even that the work is hard exactly, it’s just like everything takes so much more out of me. Even folding laundry feels like this whole production, and then I’ll get distracted by needing to match socks or realizing we’re out of detergent—sorry. But no, I don’t usually feel rested.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think so. It’s been kind of embarrassing, actually. Like it shouldn’t be hard, it’s just files, but my brain sort of stalls out.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. He’ll say I’m moving around the house like I’m underwater, like everything is slowed down.\n\nAnd sometimes he’ll ask me something simple, like where I put a bill or whether I want tea, and I know the answer, but it takes me a while to get it out. So I don’t know if that counts as not sleeping or just me being bad at going to bed.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed my sleep feels kind of off, but I haven’t really tracked it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about everyday things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it excessive. I mean, I worry about regular things—work stuff, bills, whether I forgot to reply to someone. But I feel like everyone does that, right?\n\nSometimes I’ll get stuck on something small, like whether I said the wrong thing in an email, and then I’m thinking about it while I’m unloading the dishwasher, and then I notice the mugs are all in the wrong cabinet because my husband puts them wherever—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nI guess I’m not sure where the line is between normal worrying and “excessive.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually cold when other people seem comfortable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean, I’m cold sometimes, but I’m usually the person who brings a sweater everywhere anyway. My husband runs warm, so he’s not a great comparison. Sandbags is what it feels like sometimes.\n\nAnd then I’m trying to get through work, staring at spreadsheets or survey results, and it’s not even that the work is hard exactly, it’s just like everything takes so much more out of me. Like it shouldn’t be hard, it’s just files, but my brain sort of stalls out.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. My husband has actually mentioned it, which is how I noticed it more. He’ll say I’m moving around the house like I’m underwater, like everything is slowed down.\n\nAnd sometimes he’ll ask me something simple, like where I put a bill or whether I want tea, and I know the answer, but it takes me a while to get it out. The words just kind of… come slowly. It’s frustrating, because in my head I’m not trying to be dramatic or ignore him, I’m just lagging, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, can you remember a past time when you felt this same kind of depression for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Most days I wake up and there’s just this heavy, flat feeling in my chest, like before anything has even happened. It’s more that late at night, when everything’s quiet, I catch myself thinking my husband might be better off without me. I guess I wouldn’t know unless he told me, right?\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever a time when you felt depressed or lost interest in things nearly every day for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I remember times where I’d lose interest in things and feel really low, but pinning it down to “nearly every day” and “two weeks” is hard for me.\n\nI wasn’t writing it on a calendar or anything. And sometimes I’d still go to work and answer emails and do the grocery list, so it’s like… does that count? I remember one winter I barely wanted to do anything after work, but then again it was cold and dark and we were dealing with that weird issue with the furnace—sorry, that’s probably not what you’re asking.\n\nSo… maybe, but I can’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if it’s “trouble sleeping” in the way you mean.\n\nSome nights I’m up later than I planned, but then that can happen because I get caught up scrolling or I remember some random thing I forgot to do—like last night I started thinking about whether I’d paid the water bill, and then somehow I was looking at kitchen organizers online. So I don’t know if that counts as not sleeping or just me being bad at going to bed.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed my sleep feels kind of off, but I haven’t really tracked it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about everyday things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it excessive. I mean, I worry about regular things—work stuff, bills, whether I forgot to reply to someone. But I feel like everyone does that, right?\n\nSometimes I’ll get stuck on something small, like whether I said the wrong thing in an email, and then I’m thinking about it while I’m unloading the dishwasher, and then I notice the mugs are all in the wrong cabinet because my husband puts them wherever—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nI guess I’m not sure where the line is between normal worrying and “excessive.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually cold when other people seem comfortable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? My husband runs warm, so he’s not a great comparison. And then I’ll get stuck on it, even if I try to think about something else—like whether I left dishes in the sink or if I forgot to buy coffee—and somehow it comes back to that thought.\n\nBut I haven’t planned anything. It just scares me that it keeps showing up.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time in the past when you went through a similar period of feeling this depressed?\n\nPatient: I think so… I mean, not exactly the same, but I’ve had stretches where it felt like this. Most days now I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling in my chest, like before I even get out of bed I already know the day is going to be gray.\n\nSometimes I’ll just sit at the kitchen table and stare out at the yard—there’s this one patch where the grass never really comes in right, no matter what I do, which is ridiculous because I’ve tried seed and those little sprinkler timers—and I’ll catch myself thinking there’s no good version of the future coming. Like I can’t picture things turning around.\n\nSo… yes, I guess I have felt this kind of low before, but this current stretch feels pretty hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Before this current stretch, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I know I’ve had low times before, but whether it was definitely two full weeks… I don’t know, I don’t keep track very well.\n\nI can remember thinking, “I’ve been dragging for a while,” but then I get mixed up because work would get busy, or I’d be focused on some report deadline, or my mom would call about something with her house, and I’d just sort of push through. So… maybe? But I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that you snore loudly or stop breathing in your sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I’m not really sure. My husband hasn’t said anything like that to me, I don’t think. I mean, sometimes he’ll joke that I breathe “dramatically” when I’m annoyed, but that’s during the day, not sleeping.\n\nI haven’t woken up to him shaking me or saying I stopped breathing or anything. I guess I wouldn’t know unless he told me, right?\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever a time when you felt depressed or lost interest in things nearly every day for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I remember times where I’d lose interest in things and feel really low, but pinning it down to “nearly every day” and “two weeks” is hard for me.\n\nI wasn’t writing it on a calendar or anything. And sometimes I’d still go to work and answer emails and do the grocery list, so it’s like… does that count? I remember one winter I barely wanted to do anything after work, but then again it was cold and dark and we were dealing with that weird issue with the furnace—sorry, that’s probably not what you’re asking.\n\nSo… maybe, but I can’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if it’s “trouble sleeping” in the way you mean.\n\nSome nights I’m up later than I planned, but then that can happen because I get caught up scrolling or I remember some random thing I forgot to do—like last night I started thinking about whether I’d paid the water bill, and then somehow I was looking at kitchen organizers online. So I don’t know if that counts as not sleeping or just me being bad at going to bed.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed my sleep feels kind of off, but I haven’t really tracked it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about everyday things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it excessive. I mean, I worry about regular things—work stuff, bills, whether I forgot to reply to someone. But I feel like everyone does that, right?\n\nSometimes I’ll get stuck on something small, like whether I said the wrong thing in an email, and then I’m thinking about it while I’m unloading the dishwasher, and then I notice the mugs are all in the wrong cabinet because my husband puts them wherever—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nI guess I’m not sure where the line is between normal worrying and “excessive.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually cold when other people seem comfortable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean, I’m cold sometimes, but I’m usually the person who brings a sweater everywhere anyway. Like restaurants are always freezing to me, especially those ones with the vents right over the booth—there’s this place near my office where I swear they keep it like a meat locker.\n\nBut compared to other people… I haven’t really noticed anything unusual. My husband runs warm, so he’s not a great comparison. So I’d say no, not really, or at least not that I’ve paid attention to.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up in the morning, do you usually feel rested?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Even if I’ve been in bed a full night, I wake up feeling like I didn’t get restored, if that makes sense. Like my body is just… heavy. Sandbags is what it feels like sometimes.\n\nAnd then I’m trying to get through work, staring at spreadsheets or survey results, and it’s not even that the work is hard exactly, it’s just like everything takes so much more out of me. Even folding laundry feels like this whole production, and then I’ll get distracted by needing to match socks or realizing we’re out of detergent—sorry. But no, I don’t usually feel rested.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think so. It’s been kind of embarrassing, actually. At work I’ll open a client email or look at the survey results, and I’ll read the same paragraph three times and still be like, “Wait, what did that say?” And then I start clicking around, like maybe I need coffee or maybe the font is too small—our system also has these terrible tabs that all look the same, which drives me crazy.\n\nBut even just deciding which spreadsheet to start with can feel weirdly impossible sometimes. Like it shouldn’t be hard, it’s just files, but my brain sort of stalls out.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. My husband has actually mentioned it, which is how I noticed it more. He’ll say I’m moving around the house like I’m underwater, like everything is slowed down.\n\nAnd sometimes he’ll ask me something simple, like where I put a bill or whether I want tea, and I know the answer, but it takes me a while to get it out. The words just kind of… come slowly. It’s frustrating, because in my head I’m not trying to be dramatic or ignore him, I’m just lagging, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, can you remember a past time when you felt this same kind of depression for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, definitely, but when you say “the same kind” and “two weeks in a row,” I don’t know if I can pin it down that neatly.\n\nI’m bad with timelines unless I can anchor them to something, like a project deadline or a holiday or when my niece had her graduation thing—actually that whole spring is kind of a blur. But I don’t want to say yes if it was more like a few bad days here and there, you know? So… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Most days? Um… I’d say about two months now. Maybe a little hard to measure exactly, because at first I kept thinking it was just a bad week or work stress or the weather being gross, and then suddenly I realized I’d been feeling this depressed and slowed down for… yeah, around the past couple months.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for two weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: Before this started… I mean, I’ve had times where I was down, but the thing I’m talking about now is so specific. Most mornings I wake up and it’s like there’s this heavy, flat feeling sitting in my chest.\n\nI’ll just end up at the kitchen table, looking out at the yard, and I’m not even really thinking clearly—just this sense that there isn’t some good version of the future coming. Like it all goes gray.\n\nSorry, I know you asked if it happened before for two weeks or more. I guess I’m trying to say I don’t know if I can remember another time that was like this nearly every day. This current one is the one I can really point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “worthless” is the exact word all the time, but I do get stuck telling myself I really messed things up.\n\nIt’s mostly around the mortgage decision. I keep replaying it, like, why did I think that was okay, why didn’t I push back more, why did I act like we could handle it. And then I start thinking I was selfish and reckless, like I put our whole household at risk.\n\nMy husband keeps reminding me we decided together, and logically I hear that, but it doesn’t really land. I still feel like it’s on me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing or seeing things that other people do not?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like seeing people or hearing voices, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes when I’m really tired I’ll think I heard my phone buzz or someone say my name, but then it’s nothing—honestly that happens to everybody, right? My house is kind of noisy too, the heat clicks on and the fridge makes this weird knocking sound, which my husband keeps saying we should get checked, but then we forget until it happens again at night.\n\nBut no, I don’t think I’ve been hearing or seeing things that other people don’t.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
